### 191. assistant (2026-07-06T18:43:42.492Z)

## Measured: this video WOULD have processed on a 30 fps proxy — evidence

Diagnostic on the alpha clip that timed out — **"All 1 v 1.MP4"** (1080p60, 46,080 frames, 12.8 min) — all runs on the production `rally-worker` L4 (2026-07-06):

| Variant | Frames | WASB detect | Throughput | Rallies | vs 1,800 s cap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original 1080p60 (cap lifted) | 46,080 | 2,411.6 s | 19.1 fps | 56 | ✗ times out — needs 34 % more |
| **720p30 proxy** | 23,040 | **955.9 s** | 24.1 fps | **60** | ✅ **47 % under cap** |
| 1080p30 proxy | 23,040 | 1,301.8 s | 17.7 fps | 57 | ✅ 28 % under cap |

**Why it failed:** at the *real* measured throughput (~19 fps, not the ~25 assumed) the full 1080p60 clip needs **2,412 s** but WASB self-caps at 1,800 s → it dies ~75 % through with 0 segments (the silent-success bug, separate fix). Lift the cap and it finishes → **56 rallies**.

**Why the proxy works:** the **720p30 proxy completes in 956 s — 47 % under the real 1,800 s production cap — with 60 rallies.** 1080p30 also clears it (1,302 s, 28 % under). This exact video processes via either candidate.

**Quality holds:** rally counts are stable — 56 (orig) → 57 (1080p30) → 60 (720p30), no collapse. (Your scoped golden-set F1/tolF1 gate on n=15 still governs the default flip; this single clip corroborates, doesn't replace it.)

**Lever-ordering bonus:** 720p30 and 1080p30 have identical frame counts (23,040) but 720p30 is **36 % faster** (24.1 vs 17.7 fps) — the win comes substantially from cheaper CPU decode of smaller frames, not just fewer frames. Direct evidence the pipeline is decode-bound (→ #349), and that the spatial downscale earns its place on top of the fps halving.

**Note:** both proxy runs ran **concurrently** on Cloud Run (2× L4) — validates the parallel-worker assumption behind #500 (chunking).

Recommendation unchanged from the 2026-07-05 note: GPU-ffmpeg proxy (NVDEC→scale_cuda→NVENC) behind default-OFF config, golden-gate on n=15, flip the most aggressive that holds quality. This measurement puts **720p30** forward as the strong candidate (biggest cap margin + stable rally count). A full per-stage resource & latency model (CPU/RAM/GPU + cost) accompanies this investigation.

  <div class="eyebrow">Diagnostic · #483 / #349 · alpha clip “All 1 v 1.MP4” · measured on production L4</div>
  <h1>Downsample-for-detection: resource &amp; latency model</h1>
  <p class="dek">What adding a GPU-transcode <b>proxy step</b> does to the user’s end-to-end journey — per-stage latency and CPU / RAM / GPU footprint — measured on the production <b>L4</b> <code>rally-worker</code> job. Source clip: 1080p·59.94fps·<b>46,080 frames</b>·12.8 min·2.12 GiB.</p>

  <section>
    <div class="grid2">
      <div class="verdict v-fail">
        <div class="tag">Today · no proxy</div>
        <div class="big">Fails silently</div>
        <div class="meta">2,412 s needed · killed at 1,800 s · <b>0 rallies</b> delivered</div>
      </div>
      <div class="verdict v-ok">
        <div class="tag">With 720p30 proxy</div>
        <div class="big">Succeeds — measured</div>
        <div class="meta">956 s detection · 47 % under cap · <b>60 rallies</b> cut from the original</div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <section>
    <h2><span class="n">01</span> Detection vs the 1,800 s budget <span class="chip m">measured</span></h2>
    <p class="sub">WASB processes every frame (stride-1). The full 60 fps clip overshoots the cap; both 30 fps proxies land under it — and 720p is fastest because smaller frames are cheaper to <b>decode</b> (the real bottleneck).</p>

      <div class="barrow">
        <div class="lbl">Original 1080p60<span>46,080 fr · 19.1 fps · today</span></div>
        <div class="track"><div class="fill crit" style="width:92.8%"><span class="val">2,412 s → timed out · 0 rallies</span></div><div class="cap over"></div></div>

      </div>

        <div class="lbl">1080p30 proxy<span>23,040 fr · 17.7 fps</span></div>
        <div class="track"><div class="fill ok" style="width:50.1%"><span class="val">1,302 s · 57 rallies ✓</span></div><div class="cap"></div></div>
      </div>

### 199. assistant (2026-07-06T18:46:21.161Z)

# Downsample-for-detection: end-to-end resource & latency model

**Scope:** what adding the #483 GPU-transcode proxy step does to the **user's end-to-end journey** — per-stage latency + CPU/RAM/GPU footprint — vs today's no-proxy path. All figures measured on the production `rally-worker` **L4** Cloud Run Job (2026-07-06) on the alpha clip **"All 1 v 1.MP4"** (1920×1080, 59.94 fps, 46,080 frames, 12.8 min, 2.12 GiB).

Legend: **[M]** measured · **[S]** allocated spec · **[E]** estimate.

---

## TL;DR

- **Today this clip fails:** detection needs **2,411.6 s [M]** (46,080 frames @ **19.1 fps** on L4) but WASB self-caps at **1,800 s** → dies ~75 % through, **0 rallies** (would have been **56**).
- **A 720p30 proxy succeeds — measured:** **955.9 s [M]**, **47 % under the real cap**, **60 rallies**. 1080p30 also clears it (**1,301.8 s [M]**, 28 % under, 57 rallies).
- **Quality holds:** 56 → 57 → 60 rallies across variants (no regression).
- **Transcode must be GPU (NVENC):** CPU libx264 = **18 m 26 s [M]** (untenable); GPU NVDEC→NVENC ~**36 s [M, #483 dev-box]**.
- **Decode is the bottleneck, GPU is idle:** 720p30 vs 1080p30 at **identical 23,040 frames** → 720p30 **36 % faster** (24.1 vs 17.7 fps). Same GPU forward pass → the delta is CPU decode of smaller frames. L4 <1 % utilized [M] → the case for NVDEC (#349).

---

## Measured runs

| Variant | Frames | WASB detect | Throughput | Rallies | vs 1,800 s cap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original 1080p60 (cap lifted) | 46,080 | 2,411.6 s | 19.1 fps | 56 | ✗ times out (34 % over) |
| **720p30 proxy** | 23,040 | **955.9 s** | 24.1 fps | **60** | ✅ **47 % under** |
| 1080p30 proxy | 23,040 | 1,301.8 s | 17.7 fps | 57 | ✅ 28 % under |

---

## Allocated resources [S]

| Component | vCPU | RAM | GPU |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rally-worker` (Cloud Run **Job**, per execution) | 8 | 32 GiB | 1× **L4** (24 GB); task cap 3600 s; WASB self-cap 1800 s |
| `rally-control-plane` (Cloud Run **Service**) | 2 | 8 GiB | — (min 1 always-warm) |

---

## End-to-end journey (720p30 proxy)

| # | Stage | Today (no proxy) | With 720p30 proxy | CPU | RAM | GPU |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Upload → GCS | bandwidth-bound (2.12 GiB) | same | — | — | — |
| 2 | Validate + dispatch | ~1–3 s | ~1–3 s | low | low | — |
| 3 | Cold-start + mount | ~10–20 s | ~10–20 s | low | low | — |
| 4 | Pull source | ~32–47 s | ~32–47 s | I/O | low | — |
| 5 | **Transcode (NEW)** | — | **~40–90 s** GPU · 18m26s CPU ✗ | 1–2 cores | ~1–2 GiB [E] | NVENC engine |
| 6 | **WASB detection** | 2,412 s → killed → 0 | **955.9 s ✓ · 60 seg** | 1–2 cores (bottleneck) | ~2–4 GiB / 32 [E] | **<1 % util** |
| 7 | Reel from **original** | (never reached) | ~30–120 s [E] | CPU + I/O | low | opt. |
| 8 | Deliver + notify | — | ~seconds | — | — | — |
| | **TOTAL (3–8)** | **FAILS — 0 rallies** | **≈ 18–20 min → 60 rallies** | worker: 8 vCPU · 32 GiB · 1× L4 [S] | | |

---

## Four levers

| Lever | Effect on this clip | Quality risk | GPU-seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** · fps 60→30 (proxy) | 2,412 → 1,302 s (1080p30) | golden-gate (temporal) | ~½ |
| **B** · +res 1080→720 (proxy) | 1,302 → 956 s — **36 % faster, same frames** | golden-gate (spatial) | ≤ A |
| **C** · batch/prefetch tuning | GPU <1 % used → free throughput | **none** (bit-identical) | ↓ utilization |
| **D** · GPU decode / NVDEC (**#349**) | moves decode off CPU (the bottleneck) onto the idle L4 | parity-gate (chroma) | per-frame ↓↓ |

**Ship order:** 720p30 proxy (A+B) now → tuning (C) free → NVDEC (D, #349) as the high-ceiling fix. All compose with chunking (**#500**).

---

## Cost / GPU-seconds

- Detection GPU-seconds: **2,412 → 956** (~**60 % less**) [M]. Transcode adds ~40–90 NVENC-seconds (cheap, not CUDA). Net big drop + timeout failure mode removed.
- Both proxy runs ran **concurrently** on Cloud Run (2× L4) — validates the parallel-worker assumption behind #500.

---

## Provenance & gaps

- **Measured [M]:** all runs above (`rally-worker-8tmr7` / `wh66x` / `kzjw6`), video specs, CPU transcode, source pull, <1 % GPU. GPU-transcode 36 s is the #483 dev-box note.
- **Estimates [E]:** reel-compile, peak RAM, transcode CPU/RAM — firmable from Cloud Monitoring / the Ops Agent per-execution series, which #501's setup script makes routine. RAM over-provisioned (32 GiB allocated vs ~2–4 GiB used).

### 214. user (2026-07-06T18:57:21.844Z)

1	# runlogs/ — sample runs & deploy reports for posterity
2	
3	This directory is the **durable record of real runs** of the compute-decoupled serving profiles —
4	the same convention proven by the archived `DECOUPLED_COMPUTE/DEPLOY_REPORT_GCP_2026-06-20.md`. The
5	goal: **the next deploy is one-shot**, and anyone can trace exactly what was run, what it cost, and
6	what came out.
7	
8	## When to add a file here
9	- Any **real-hardware run** (owner-side L5 in [`../TESTING_STRATEGY.md`](../TESTING_STRATEGY.md)) of a profile.
10	- Every **default flip** that touches the core ships its Launch Report record here too (per [[launch-report-convention]]).
11	
12	## Naming
13	`DEPLOY_REPORT_<profile>_<YYYY-MM-DD>.md` — e.g. `DEPLOY_REPORT_truly-local_2026-07-01.md`,
14	`DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_2026-07-01.md`.
15	
16	## Required contents (mirror the DECOUPLED deploy report)
17	1. **TL;DR** — what ran, profile, ✅/⏳/⛔ per stage (detect / window / stitch / serve).
18	2. **Resources** — exact machine/VM/Cloud-Run config, region, image; for cloud, the bucket + SA + budget.
19	3. **Exact commands** — copy-pasteable (`worker infer …`, the dispatch call, the config/profile used).
20	4. **Run results** — rally counts, **profile-parity numbers** (windows/segments vs the other profile),
21	   and the metering trio **`gpu_active_seconds` / `wall_seconds` / `bytes_out`** (even before the M8 ledger lands).
22	5. **Reel smoke-test** — `ffmpeg -f null -` full decode (exit 0) + a 5-frame contact sheet visually
23	   confirming real rallies (court/net/players mid-rally).
24	6. **Gotchas** — conda-ToS, WASB-C3, GPU stockouts, font fallback, cold-start, NVDEC, etc.
25	7. **Cost** — measured/estimated; reconcile probe metrics vs the platform's per-invocation billing.
26	8. **Teardown / final state** — for cloud, the `$0` audit (`gcloud compute instances list` + `disks list` empty); **DELETE, never just stop** ([[gcp-vm-always-delete]]).
27	
28	## DoD coupling
29	`COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING` is not DONE until **`truly-local` (M5)** and **`cloud-serving` (M7)** each
30	have **≥1 committed report here** (README §9).
31	
32	## Index (append as runs land)
33	| Date | Profile | Result | File |
34	|---|---|---|---|
35	| — | — | *(none yet — M5/M7 will add the first two)* | — |
36

47	## 5. Findings (honest — two real ones)
48	
49	1. **Composite originals bypass the submit-time cache (documented P4 boundary, now measured).**
50	   `videos/MahadevpuraSingles.MP4` was bucket-uploaded as a **composite object**
51	   (`component_count=4`) → GCS exposes **no `md5Hash`** → `submit_time_video_id` returns None →
52	   the instant-done path can't fire. By itself that would only degrade to the drain-tier DB
53	   check; combined with (2) it re-dispatched.
54	2. **Fresh-DB + composite-original ⇒ the bucket cache is never consulted on the drain path.**
55	   Cloud Run replaced the rev-00013 instance between the replay query and the submit
56	   (`MANUAL_OR_CUSTOMER_MIN_INSTANCE` churn in the logs) → the drain hashed on an empty DB,
57	   found no video row, and dispatched — even though `outputs/<md5>/report.json` existed. The
58	   drain checks only the DB after hashing; the bucket tier lives on the submit path (md5-known)
59	   and the read path. **Residual gap, bounded:** it needs BOTH a composite/md5-less source AND
60	   an instance swap; browser-staged uploads (the tester flow) always carry `md5Hash` and take
61	   the instant path. Follow-up filed on #484: consult `cached_process_result` on the drain
62	   after hashing. Cost of the encounter here: one L4 re-detection (~US$0.4), harvested as the
63	   fresh-detection proof below.
64

74	  not re-driven live in this window.

### 217. assistant (2026-07-06T18:58:52.690Z)

# DEPLOY REPORT — cloud-serving, alpha-tester run "All 1 v 1", 2026-07-06

> Durable record of a real **alpha-user-tester** upload on the `cloud-serving` profile (`compute_target=local_gpu` on the `rally-worker` L4 Cloud Run Job, project `gen-lang-client-0306026826`, region `asia-southeast1`). The tester's clip **timed out and returned 0 rallies**; this run diagnoses the root cause and proves the downsample fix on the real serving path. Honest-limits in §6.

## 1. TL;DR

| Stage | Result |
|---|---|
| Ingest / dispatch | ✅ upload staged, `rally-worker` dispatched (exec `rally-worker-wf294`) |
| **Detect** | ⛔ **timed out at the 1,800 s WASB cap → 0 rallies** (then reported `status:"success"` — the silent-success bug) |
| Window / stitch / serve | ⛔ never reached (0 segments) |

**Root cause (measured):** the clip is **1080p·59.94 fps·46,080 frames·12.8 min·2.12 GiB**. WASB native detection runs at **19.1 fps** on the L4, so it needs **2,411.6 s** — but `indexing.wasb.timeout_sec=1800` kills it ~75 % through. **Fix proven on the same L4:** a **720p30 proxy** detects in **955.9 s (47 % under the cap) → 60 rallies.**

## 2. Resources

- **Worker:** `rally-worker` Cloud Run **Job**, image `asia-southeast1-docker.pkg.dev/gen-lang-client-0306026826/rally/rally-worker:latest`, **8 vCPU / 32 GiB / 1× NVIDIA L4**, task timeout 3600 s, GCSFuse mount of `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving`, SA `492532266377-compute@…`. Weights `WASB_WEIGHTS=/gcs/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar`.
- **Diagnostic VM (transcode only, now deleted):** `rally-diag483`, `g2-standard-4` (L4), `asia-southeast1-a`, DL image `common-cu129-ubuntu-2204-nvidia-580`.
- **Alpha source:** `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/uploads/d2d89b44d9e0497a97ca9a6c8ce4a19d/All 1 v 1.MP4`.

## 3. Exact commands

```bash
# Baseline — full original, DEFAULT settings, WASB cap lifted (proves it completes if uncapped)
gcloud run jobs execute rally-worker --region=asia-southeast1 --project=gen-lang-client-0306026826 --async \
  --args='^@@^infer@@--video-uri=gs://…/uploads/d2d89b44…/All 1 v 1.MP4@@--out-uri=gs://khelsutra-rally-serving@@--segmenter=wasb_hybrid@@--set=deployment.compute_target=local_gpu@@--set=indexing.detector_impl=native@@--set=indexing.skip_ai_handoff=true@@--set=indexing.wasb.timeout_sec=0'

# Proxy transcodes (on the diag VM; CPU libx264 — NVENC unavailable, see §6)
ffmpeg -i in.mp4 -vf "fps=30"              -c:v libx264 -preset veryfast -crf 20 -c:a copy p1080p30.mp4
ffmpeg -i in.mp4 -vf "fps=30,scale=1280:720" -c:v libx264 -preset veryfast -crf 20 -c:a copy p720p30.mp4

# Proxy detection — REAL 1,800 s cap kept (completing under it = proof)
gcloud run jobs execute rally-worker … --args='…--video-uri=…/diag483-720p30/…720p30.MP4…--out-uri=gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/diag483/p720p30…'   # wh66x
gcloud run jobs execute rally-worker … --args='…--video-uri=…/diag483-1080p30/…1080p30.MP4…--out-uri=gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/diag483/p1080p30…' # kzjw6
```

## 4. Run results (metering trio: `gpu_active_seconds` / `wall_seconds` / `bytes_out`)

| Run (exec) | Variant | Frames | Detect `gpu_active_s` | `wall_s` | Throughput | Rallies | vs 1,800 s cap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `wf294` (alpha prod) | 1080p60, cap 1800 | 46,080 | 1,800 (killed) | 1,928 | — | **0** ⛔ | ✗ timed out |
| `8tmr7` (baseline) | 1080p60, cap off | 46,080 | 2,411.6 | 2,532 | 19.1 fps | 56 | ✗ 34 % over |
| **`wh66x`** | **720p30** | 23,040 | **955.9** | 1,017 | 24.1 fps | **60** | ✅ 47 % under |
| `kzjw6` | 1080p30 | 23,040 | 1,301.8 | 1,386 | 17.7 fps | 57 | ✅ 28 % under |

- `bytes_out`: `outputs/<md5>/{intervals.csv, report.json}` per run (KB-scale; no reel — detection-only, see §5).
- **Parity / quality:** rally counts stable across variants — **56 → 57 → 60** (no collapse). Rally-level, not byte-level (cross-config detection is sub-pixel-different by design).
- **Decode-bound proof:** 720p30 vs 1080p30 at **identical 23,040 frames** → 720p30 **36 % faster** (24.1 vs 17.7 fps); same GPU forward pass, so the delta is CPU decode of smaller frames. L4 measured **<1 % utilized** → see #349.

## 5. Reel smoke-test

**N/A for this run** — all four executions were **detection-only** (`skip_ai_handoff=true`, no in-process stitch), so no reel artifact was produced. The 60-rally 720p30 result is the artifact of record; reel compilation from the **original** (full quality) is a downstream stage not exercised here.

## 6. Gotchas / honest limits

- **Silent-success (now fixed):** the timed-out prod run wrote `report.json status:"success", segment_count:0`. Fixed in **#503** (`bd5647b`, merged) — timeouts now surface as failed.
- **No NVENC on the DL-VM image** → proxy transcode fell back to CPU libx264 at **18 m 26 s** (vs the ~36 s GPU NVDEC→NVENC dev-box number). Motivates a checked-in VM setup script (**#501**).
- **`--max-frames` was ignored** by the wasb_hybrid path (baseline ran full) — minor; noted for the worker CLI.
- Windows `gcloud compute ssh` uses PuTTY `plink`/`pscp` (rejects `-o` flags, mishandles `~`).
- **Not proven here:** the golden-set (n=15) F1/tolF1 gate for the proxy default-flip (#483) — this single clip corroborates, it does not replace that gate; and the end-to-end reel/serve stages.

## 7. Cost

Rough (M8 ledger not landed): Cloud Run L4 `gpu_active` ≈ 1,800 + 2,412 + 956 + 1,302 ≈ **6,470 s ≈ 1.8 L4-hours**, plus the diag VM ≈ 1 L4-hour (g2-standard-4). At ~US$0.85 / L4-hour on-demand ⇒ **≈ US$2.4** for the whole diagnostic session. The proxy path is **~50 % fewer detection GPU-seconds** per clip going forward.

## 8. Teardown / final state ($0 audit)

- Diag VM **deleted** (never stopped): `gcloud compute instances list` → **0 items**; `gcloud compute disks list` → **0 items** (no orphan boot disk). ✅ $0.
- `rally-worker` job + `rally-control-plane` service unchanged.

## 9. Outcomes riding this session

- Issues filed: **#500** (chunked/parallel detection), **#501** (canonical VM setup script). Comments with measured evidence on **#483** (downsample proxy) and **#349** (NVDEC GPU decode).
- **#503** silent-success fix merged.
- NVDEC/#349 picked up as a tracked project (default-OFF, parity-gated).
- Full per-stage **resource & latency model** produced (CPU/RAM/GPU + cost).

The `spawn_task` chip is a **Claude Code desktop-app** element — it appears in the app's task/suggestions tray, and clicking it opens a *separate* session in a fresh git worktree on your machine. I can't press it for you (it's a local UI gesture), and it won't be actionable from a web/other client. So "authorize me to do it here" is exactly the right call when the chip isn't reachable — which is what we just did.

- 🔄 **Decode-library feasibility spike is running** (background): which GPU-decode lib actually fits the pinned `torch 1.11.0+cu113 / py3.8 / cu113` env — OpenCV-CUDA vs ffmpeg-cuvid vs DALI vs PyNvVideoCodec vs torchvision-GPU vs decord. This is the gating question (env packaging is the real risk), so I'm resolving it before writing code.
- **Next, once it reports:** branch from `origin/master`, add a `decode_backend: cpu|nvdec` flag (default `cpu`) with a CPU-path-preserving loader abstraction in `wasb_infer.py` + tests + a session-handoff, and open a draft PR referencing #349.

# Research Spike: NVDEC decode backend for WASB shuttle-detector (issue #349)

## 1. The loader contract the decode path must satisfy

Read from `backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py` (absolute: `C:\Users\avidu\Projects\khelsutra-guru\badminton-highlight-indexer\backend\pipeline\detectors\wasb_infer.py`):

| Element | Location | Contract |
|---|---|---|
| `make_video_frame_loader` | L743–783 | Returns `(frame_loader, count_hint, release)`. `frame_loader(index) -&gt; BGR uint8 ndarray`, **forward-only**, `cv2.VideoCapture` + `cap.set(CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES)` seek only on resume. |
| `SequentialFrameStore` | L269–316 | Sliding buffer, window = `frames_in`; calls `reader(index)` with **non-decreasing** indices; keeps O(window) frames. |
| Frame→tensor | `_bgr_to_pil_rgb` L328–340 | `cv2.cvtColor(bgr, COLOR_BGR2RGB)` → `PIL.Image.fromarray` → RGB PIL image. Engineered to be **byte-identical** to the PNG-on-disk path (`cv2.imwrite` PNG → `Image.open().convert('RGB')`), "verified empirically, max|diff|=0". |
| `read_image` shim | L343–397 | Rebinds `dataloaders.dataset_loader.read_image` to feed the in-memory PIL frame into WASB's **unmodified** `ImageDataset.__getitem__`. |
| Resize + normalize | inside WASB repo (`build_img_transforms`/`ImageDataset`, external) | **Resize to `inp_width×inp_height` happens on the CPU PIL frame, downstream of the loader.** The loader hands off full-resolution BGR. |
| Batching | `DataLoader(shuffle=False, num_workers=0)` + `_PrefetchIter` L403–445 | Streaming forces `num_workers=0` (in-process shim can't be pickled). `_PrefetchIter` overlaps CPU batch-assembly with GPU inference via one producer thread. |
| GPU hand-off | `detector.run_tensor(imgs, trans)` L646 | Receives a **CPU** tensor batch (assembled by the DataLoader from PIL via WASB transforms) + affine `trans`; moves `imgs` to GPU internally. |

**Two constraints dominate the whole evaluation:**

1. **Parity boundary is above the loader.** The contract is "return a BGR uint8 ndarray identical to what a lossless PNG round-trip yields." Any NVDEC backend produces YUV/NV12 that must be color-converted to **BGR bit-identically to `cv2`'s H.264 SW-decode path** — the hard part. NVDEC's built-in YUV→RGB (BT.601/709 matrix, limited vs. full range, chroma upsampling) will **not** match libavcodec/cv2 pixel-for-pixel. This is the central risk, not raw compatibility.
2. **The GPU tensor never reaches `run_tensor` from the loader.** The loader returns a host ndarray; resize/ToTensor run on CPU inside WASB; only then does WASB move to GPU. So "keep frames on GPU" gives **no** end-to-end win *without also rewriting WASB's transform/collate path* — which is out of scope ("Do NOT change code") and would blow the parity guarantee. An NVDEC backend that decodes on GPU then `hwdownload`s to a host BGR ndarray is the only drop-in shape.

## 2. Compatibility table (pinned env: torch 1.11.0+cu113, torchvision 0.12.0+cu113, py3.8, conda/micromamba on `nvidia/cuda:12.4.1-runtime`, L4, host driver 580 / CUDA 12.x)

| Backend | Works w/ py3.8 + cu113 torch 1.11? | Install friction (conda/micromamba) | Frames stay on GPU? | Sequential + resize + CUDA-tensor fit | Maintenance | Parity risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ffmpeg `-hwaccel cuda`/`h264_cuvid`, hwdownload → host BGR** (subprocess pipe or PyAV) | ✅ Independent of torch; ffmpeg already in image | ✅ Low — ffmpeg present; `av` is a pip wheel; or pure subprocess | ❌ By design downloads to host (that's the point for drop-in) | ✅✅ Best — returns BGR ndarray, WASB resizes as today | ✅ ffmpeg active; PyAV active | **Medium** — NVDEC YUV→BGR ≠ cv2 SW-decode exactly; **must parity-gate** |
| **NVIDIA DALI** (`fn.experimental.decoders.video` / video reader) | ⚠️ py3.8 only ≤ DALI **1.49**; use `nvidia-dali-cuda110` (CUDA 11.0 enhanced-compat, driver ≥450.80 → fine on 580) | ⚠️ Medium — pinned old wheel from NVIDIA index; heavy dep | ✅ Yes (GPU pipeline) | ⚠️ Pipeline/graph model, not a random-seek `reader(i)`; sequential OK but seek-resume awkward; DLPack→torch | ✅ Active (but 1.49 branch frozen for py3.8) | **High** — its own color/resize; hard to match cv2 |
| **PyNvVideoCodec** (NVIDIA NVDEC bindings) | ✅ py≥3.8, ready PyPI wheels; runtime-linked to driver (580 ok) | ✅ Low — `pip install PyNvVideoCodec` | ✅ Yes; DLPack/CAI → torch | ⚠️ Decode API is stream/seek-capable; you own YUV→RGB→BGR + download | ✅ NVIDIA-maintained (VPF successor) | **High** — you implement color conversion; matching cv2 is on you |
| **OpenCV `cv2.cudacodec.VideoReader`** | ⚠️ Not in standard `opencv-python`; env already has **opencv-python-headless** (no CUDA). Need source build or `cudawarped/opencv-python-cuda-wheels`; must match py3.8 + a CUDA the wheel was built against | ❌ High — replace the pinned headless build; risk clashing with WASB's cv2 usage | ❌ `nextFrame()` returns a `GpuMat`; download to get ndarray | ⚠️ API close to cv2 but GpuMat, not ndarray | ⚠️ Community wheels (cudawarped) active; core feature stable | **High** — cudacodec color path ≠ cv2 CPU path |
| **torchvision.io GPU `VideoReader(device="cuda")`** | ❌ **GPU decoder merged Dec 2021, first shipped ~0.13; `0.12.0` predates it.** Even later, **not compiled into PyPI wheels** (needs source build w/ Video Codec SDK). Known color/shape/**seek** bugs (#7745). Deprecated 0.22, removed 0.26 | ❌ Very high — would force torch/torchvision bump = **env bump** | ✅ (when built) | ❌ seek unreliable (#7745) — breaks resume | ❌ Deprecated → removed; superseded by TorchCodec | **High** + broken |
| **decord (GPU)** | ❌ PyPI ships **CPU-only**; GPU needs `-DUSE_CUDA=ON` **source build**. Last release **v0.6.0 (2022)** — effectively unmaintained | ❌ High — source build; forks (eva-decord, decord2) don't ship GPU wheels either | ✅ (when built) `bridge_to_torch` | ⚠️ has seek, but stale | ❌ Stale since 2022 | **High** |

## 3. Ranked recommendation

**Primary: ffmpeg NVDEC with `hwdownload` to a host BGR ndarray** (behind `decode_backend="nvdec"`, default `"cpu"`), preferring a **subprocess `ffmpeg` pipe of `rawvideo bgr24`** over PyAV.

Rationale:
- Zero impact on the pinned wheel set — it's a separate process / a pip `av` wheel; **cannot force an env bump.** ffmpeg is already installed in both deploy images.
- Maps cleanly onto the loader: it still returns a **BGR uint8 ndarray**, so `SequentialFrameStore` → `_bgr_to_pil_rgb` → WASB transforms/resize run **completely unchanged**. This is the only option that preserves the byte-parity architecture without touching WASB.
- Forward-only sequential read is exactly ffmpeg's happy path; resume = restart the pipe with `-ss`/frame-select at `start_index`.
- The GPU→host copy is deliberate and acceptable: the WASB path already needs a host frame (resize is CPU-side), and NVDEC still offloads the expensive H.264 entropy/motion-comp decode from CPU to the L4's dedicated decoder block.

**Fallback: PyNvVideoCodec** (NVIDIA-maintained, clean py3.8 wheel, driver-linked so the cu113/driver-580 split is irrelevant to it). Choose this only if profiling shows the hwdownload copy is the bottleneck and you're willing to own a GPU-resident path — which requires rewriting WASB's collate/transform to accept a CUDA tensor and **re-proving parity**, i.e. more than a loader swap.

**Explicitly rejected for this env:**
- **torchvision GPU VideoReader** — not in 0.12.0 at all; would force a torch/torchvision bump (**env-bump flag**), and has unreliable seek (#7745) that breaks resume.
- **decord GPU** — CPU-only wheels, source build required, unmaintained since 2022.
- **OpenCV cudacodec** — requires ripping out the pinned `opencv-python-headless` for a CUDA build; high risk of clashing with WASB's own cv2 use.
- **DALI** — frozen on 1.49 for py3.8, and its graph/own-color model fights both the `reader(i)` seek contract and the byte-parity requirement.

## 4. Integration sketch against `wasb_infer.py`'s loader (default-OFF, parity-gated)

Add a factory that dispatches on a new `decode_backend` flag; **do not touch** `SequentialFrameStore`, `_bgr_to_pil_rgb`, or the shim — the whole point is that the NVDEC loader satisfies the *identical* `frame_loader(index) -&gt; BGR uint8 ndarray` contract.

```python
# new, additive — sits beside make_video_frame_loader (L743)
def make_video_frame_loader(video_path, decode_backend="cpu"):   # default cpu
    if decode_backend == "cpu":
        return _make_cpu_frame_loader(video_path)        # today's cv2 body, unchanged
    elif decode_backend == "nvdec":
        return _make_nvdec_frame_loader(video_path)      # ffmpeg -hwaccel cuda, bgr24 out
    raise SystemExit(f"unknown decode_backend {decode_backend!r}")
```

`_make_nvdec_frame_loader` returns the same 3-tuple `(frame_loader, count_hint, release)`:
- `count_hint`: keep `cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT` probe (`_probe_frame_count`, L731) so the frame list is identical.
- `frame_loader(index)`: hold a long-lived `ffmpeg -hwaccel cuda -hwaccel_output_format cuda -i … -vf "hwdownload,format=bgr24" -f rawvideo -pix_fmt bgr24 pipe:1` process; read `H*W*3` bytes → `np.frombuffer(...).reshape(H, W, 3)`. Forward-only reads pull the next frame; a resume (`index &gt; pos`) re-spawns the process seeking to `index` (or select frames), mirroring the existing `cap.set(POS_FRAMES)` seek-once semantics (L770–773).
- `release()`: terminate the ffmpeg process.

Thread the `decode_backend` param up through `run_video_streaming` (L786) → `make_video_frame_loader`, defaulting `"cpu"` everywhere, and expose it as a CLI/set flag. Nothing downstream of the loader changes.

**Parity gate (mandatory before default-ON):** a test that decodes N frames both ways and asserts the WASB **network-input tensors** match within tolerance (a small ε, not `max|diff|=0` — NVDEC color conversion will differ by a few LSB from cv2's SW path). Gate default-ON on either byte-parity OR an end-to-end trajectory-CSV equivalence within the detector's tolerance. **If exact parity fails**, the flag stays opt-in and any consumer must accept a documented pixel delta.

## 5. Top risks / open questions

1. **cu113-vs-driver split is a non-issue for the primary pick, real for others.** ffmpeg and PyNvVideoCodec link NVDEC through the **driver** (580, CUDA 12.x) at runtime — independent of the wasb env's cu113 torch. DALI's CUDA-11.0 enhanced-compat wheel also runs on driver ≥450.80. Only torchvision-GPU / cudacodec / decord-GPU would drag CUDA into the *build*, and those are already rejected. **No candidate in the recommendation forces an env bump — flag confirmed clear.**
2. **Color/chroma parity is THE risk.** NVDEC YUV→RGB (BT.601 vs 709, limited vs full range, chroma-siting/upsampling) will not match libavcodec's SW decode (what `cv2.VideoCapture` uses) bit-for-bit. The repo's parity guarantee (`max|diff|=0`) almost certainly **will not hold**; the realistic bar is "detector output within tolerance." Open question: is the detector's trajectory output stable under a few-LSB pixel delta? Needs the parity harness to answer.
3. **hwdownload negates the "stay on GPU" benefit — accepted deliberately.** True GPU-resident hand-off would need WASB's transform/collate rewritten to resize on-GPU and pass a CUDA tensor to `run_tensor` — out of scope and parity-breaking. Flag if a future issue wants the fully-GPU path.
4. **Codec coverage.** Confirm source clips are H.264/HEVC that L4 NVDEC supports (it does for H.264/HEVC/AV1); VP9/odd profiles or B-frame/variable-fps edge cases can desync frame indexing vs. the `cv2` count. Validate `count_hint` equals actual decoded frames on a sample.
5. **Resume seek exactness.** The CPU path relies on `cap.set(CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES)` frame-accurate seek; ffmpeg `-ss` is timestamp-based and can land off-by-frames on VFR/open-GOP content. The NVDEC loader must seek **frame-accurately** (decode-and-discard to `index`, or `-ss` before `-i` + trim) to keep resume identical.
6. **Wheel availability drift.** `nvidia-dali-cuda110`'s current PyPI metadata already dropped py3.8 (needs the pinned ≤1.49 from NVIDIA's own index) — a reminder that any pinned-old-wheel fallback is one yank away from breaking. The primary pick (ffmpeg subprocess) has no such fragility.

**Sources:** [PyNvVideoCodec docs](https://docs.nvidia.com/video-technologies/pynvvideocodec/read-me/index.html), [PyNvVideoCodec PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/PyNvVideoCodec/), [torchvision GPU decoder PR #5019](https://github.com/pytorch/vision/pull/5019), [torchvision #6161 "Not compiled with GPU decoder support"](https://github.com/pytorch/vision/issues/6161), [torchvision #7745 cuda backend shape/color/seek](https://github.com/pytorch/vision/issues/7745), [torchvision video deprecation (0.22 docs)](https://docs.pytorch.org/vision/0.22/io.html), [DALI installation / enhanced compatibility](https://docs.nvidia.com/deeplearning/dali/user-guide/docs/installation.html), [DALI releases (py3.8 ≤1.49)](https://github.com/NVIDIA/DALI/releases), [CUDA forward/enhanced compatibility](https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/cuda-compatibility/latest/), [decord repo (PyPI CPU-only, build from source for GPU)](https://github.com/dmlc/decord), [decord releases (last v0.6.0)](https://github.com/dmlc/decord/releases), [cudawarped/opencv-python-cuda-wheels](https://github.com/cudawarped/opencv-python-cuda-wheels/releases), [OpenCV cudacodec::VideoReader ref](https://docs.opencv.org/4.x/db/ded/classcv_1_1cudacodec_1_1VideoReader.html), [PyAV h264_cuvid #451](https://github.com/mikeboers/PyAV/issues/451), [PyAV DLPack/CUDA PR #2155](https://github.com/PyAV-Org/PyAV/pull/2155), [NVIDIA FFmpeg transcoding guide (hwdownload behavior)](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-ffmpeg-transcoding-guide/).</result>

### 231. assistant (2026-07-06T19:02:37.924Z)

The spike came back decisive — and it surfaced a nuance that's a genuine decision point, so I'll hold here as I said I would.

## Library pick: **ffmpeg NVDEC → host BGR ndarray**, behind a `decode_backend: cpu|nvdec` flag (default `cpu`)
- `ffmpeg -hwaccel cuda -hwaccel_output_format cuda -vf "hwdownload,format=bgr24"` via a subprocess pipe, returning the same `BGR uint8 ndarray` the loader contract already requires.
- **Crucially: no env bump.** ffmpeg links NVDEC through the *driver* (580/CUDA 12.x) at runtime, independent of the pinned `torch 1.11+cu113 / py3.8` env. Every option that would drag CUDA into the *build* (torchvision-GPU — not even in 0.12.0; OpenCV-cudacodec; decord-GPU; DALI) was rejected for exactly that reason.

## The nuance that changes the calculus (worth your call)
The spike read the actual loader and found: **WASB resizes each frame to the network input on the CPU, *downstream* of the loader**, and the frame is handed off as a host ndarray. So a drop-in NVDEC backend must `hwdownload` back to host — which means:

- **NVDEC offloads only the H.264 decode**, not the CPU color-convert + resize. It's a *real but partial* win. The full GPU win would need rewriting WASB's transform/collate to resize on-GPU and pass a CUDA tensor — a much bigger change that also breaks the byte-parity guarantee. Out of scope.
- **Parity is the real risk:** NVDEC's YUV→BGR won't match libavcodec's software decode bit-for-bit, so the repo's `max|diff|=0` guarantee almost certainly breaks; the realistic bar is "detector output within tolerance," which needs a parity harness to confirm.

**Why this matters strategically:** the **720p30 proxy (#483) is the bigger, proven, lower-risk lever** — shrinking the frames cuts decode *and* resize, which is exactly why it gave the clean 47%-under-cap result. NVDEC is the higher-effort complement whose payoff is partial and gated on chroma parity. So it's less of a slam-dunk than "just move decode to the GPU" first sounded.

## My recommendation
Land the **cheap, no-GPU, de-risking part now** and gate the rest on measurement:
1. Branch + add the `decode_backend` flag with the ffmpeg-NVDEC loader beside the CPU one (default `cpu`, CPU path untouched) + a **parity harness** (decode N frames both ways, compare network-input tensors) — all no-GPU, draft PR to #349.
2. **Hold enablement** until the parity harness runs on an L4 and shows the trajectory output holds within tolerance *and* the decode-offload actually moves the throughput needle (given resize stays on CPU).
3. Post this spike as the implementation plan on **#349** so the decision is recorded.

**Your call** — want me to:
- **(A)** proceed with steps 1+3 now (scaffold + parity harness + draft PR + post to #349), holding GPU enablement for your go; or
- **(B)** just post the spike to #349 and **park NVDEC behind #483**, since the proxy is the proven win and NVDEC's is partial; or
- **(C)** go all the way including the GPU parity/throughput run (spins up an L4, ~small $)?

I'd pick **(A)** — it captures the value and de-risks parity without committing GPU spend or touching serving.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# NVDEC experiment box setup: static ffmpeg (nvdec+nvenc) + opencv + the alpha clip.
set -uo pipefail
mkdir -p ~/nvdec && cd ~/nvdec
LOG=~/nvdec/setup.log; : > "$LOG"; exec > >(tee -a "$LOG") 2>&1
echo "=== $(date -u) SETUP START ==="
nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,driver_version --format=csv,noheader

# 1. static ffmpeg with nvdec + nvenc (BtbN gpl build; links NVDEC/NVENC via the driver at runtime)
if [ ! -x ~/nvdec/ffmpeg ]; then
  echo "[ff] downloading static ffmpeg (BtbN gpl)..."
  curl -sSL -o ff.tar.xz https://github.com/BtbN/FFmpeg-Builds/releases/download/latest/ffmpeg-master-latest-linux64-gpl.tar.xz
  tar xf ff.tar.xz
  d=$(ls -d ffmpeg-*-linux64-gpl 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  cp "$d/bin/ffmpeg" "$d/bin/ffprobe" ~/nvdec/ && chmod +x ~/nvdec/ffmpeg ~/nvdec/ffprobe
  rm -rf ff.tar.xz "$d"
fi
echo "[ff] hwaccels:"; ~/nvdec/ffmpeg -hide_banner -hwaccels 2>&1 | tr '\n' ' '; echo
echo "[ff] cuda/cuvid decoders:"; ~/nvdec/ffmpeg -hide_banner -decoders 2>&1 | grep -iE "cuvid|_cuda|nvdec" | head
echo "[ff] nvenc encoders:"; ~/nvdec/ffmpeg -hide_banner -encoders 2>&1 | grep -iE "nvenc" | head

# 2. python: opencv (CPU/SW-decode reference) + numpy (parity math)
command -v pip3 >/dev/null 2>&1 || { sudo apt-get update -qq && sudo DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install -y -qq python3-pip; }
python3 -c "import cv2" 2>/dev/null || pip3 -q install --user opencv-python-headless numpy
python3 -c "import cv2,numpy; print('[py] cv2',cv2.__version__,'numpy',numpy.__version__)"

# 3. pull the alpha clip (in-region)
echo "[dl] pulling alpha clip..."
time gsutil -q cp "gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/uploads/d2d89b44d9e0497a97ca9a6c8ce4a19d/All 1 v 1.MP4" in.mp4
ls -la in.mp4
echo "[probe] color metadata (governs the 'correct' YUV->BGR conversion):"
~/nvdec/ffprobe -v error -select_streams v:0 \
  -show_entries stream=codec_name,width,height,nb_frames,pix_fmt,color_range,color_space,color_primaries,color_transfer \
  -of default in.mp4
echo "=== $(date -u) SETUP DONE ==="; touch ~/nvdec/SETUP_DONE

### 246. assistant (2026-07-06T19:36:27.100Z)

## Why

We have no release process, and it's starting to bite: a change lands, the image is rebuilt as `:latest`, and there's no durable line tying "what shipped" to a git ref, a version, or an image digest. Concretely, today:

- **1 git tag total** — `_pre_sync_m4_44c9f01` (a sync marker, **not** a release). No semver tags.
- **No `CHANGELOG`, no `VERSION`**, `pyproject.toml` still at the default `version = "0.1.0"` (never bumped).
- The worker/control-plane image is only ever `…/rally-worker:latest` — **a deploy can't be traced back to a git SHA or a version**, only to a digest recorded ad-hoc in a runlog.

The good news: history already uses **conventional commits with PR numbers** (`feat:`/`fix:`/`docs:` … `(#NNN)`), the build is one config (`deploy/cloudrun/cloudbuild.yaml`), and the `docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/` `DEPLOY_REPORT_*` convention already records live deploys. So bootstrapping a real process is mostly wiring, not invention.

## This issue tracks: the next release + the process to cut it

### Part A — Bootstrap a lightweight release process

1. **Versioning (semver).** Bump `pyproject.toml`; tag the released ref `vX.Y.Z`. Surface the version at runtime (extend `/api/health` + `/api/capabilities`, which already return build-ish fields, with `app_version` + the git short-SHA).
2. **CHANGELOG.md.** Generate from conventional commits since the last tag (grouped feat/fix/docs, with the `(#NNN)` links). The history already supports this — no back-fill needed beyond the first cut.
3. **Traceable images.** Tag the built image with **`:vX.Y.Z` and `:<git-sha>`** (not just `:latest`) in `cloudbuild.yaml`; record the resolved **digest** in the DEPLOY_REPORT runlog (already the convention). A release is then ref → version → digest, end to end.
4. **Release checklist / DoD** (reuse the existing pre-LGTM gate + runlog convention): on the tagged ref — full test suite green, `ruff`/`mypy` clean, coverage ≥ CI floor; CHANGELOG updated; image built + tagged (version + sha) + digest pinned on `rally-worker`; CP deployed via `gen_service_yaml.py`; `DEPLOY_REPORT_cloud-serving_<date>.md` committed; smoke test (health 200 + one real detection). 
5. **Cadence.** Cut a release per meaningful batch of merged PRs, and always at a default-flip / Launch Report.

### Part B — Cut **v0.1.0** now (the jumpstart)

Establish the baseline with a first tagged release capturing everything already merged (the CP-durable-rebuild sweep #490/#491/#492/#493/#494/#495, the silent-success worker fix **#503**, alpha-readiness docs #498/#499, and the alpha-tester runlog #504). This gives us a real `v0.1.0` to diff future releases against and proves the checklist end to end.

### Part C — Candidate contents for the *following* release

The GPU-cost workstream, gated as each lands: **#349** (NVDEC decode — cheap win in progress), **#483** (downsample proxy — measured, golden-gate pending), **#500** (chunked/parallel detection), **#501** (canonical VM setup script).

## References
- Build: `deploy/cloudrun/cloudbuild.yaml` (currently emits `:latest` only). Deploy: `deploy/cloudrun/gen_service_yaml.py`. Runlogs: `docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/runlogs/` (`DEPLOY_REPORT_*`). Gate: the pre-LGTM checklist in `CLAUDE.md`.

## Open questions
- Tag on `master` per release vs a `release/*` branch? (Given concurrent-session branching discipline, tagging `master` at a chosen SHA is simplest.)
- Auto-generate the CHANGELOG in CI (e.g. git-cliff / release-please) vs a scripted one-shot? Start scripted, automate later.
- Do we want `v0.1.0` or a date-based scheme? (Semver recommended — the API already speaks a `ui_version` hash for the SPA; app semver complements it.)

### 253. assistant (2026-07-06T19:40:23.032Z)

#!/usr/bin/env python3
# NVDEC vs CPU decode: E1 throughput + E2 YUV->BGR color parity. Alpha clip, L4.
import subprocess, time, sys, numpy as np, cv2
FF="/home/avidu/nvdec/ffmpeg"; IN="/home/avidu/nvdec/in.mp4"; W,H=1920,1080
N=2000   # E1 frames
M=200    # E2 frames

def sh(cmd):
    t=time.time()
    r=subprocess.run(cmd,stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,stderr=subprocess.PIPE)
    return time.time()-t, r.returncode, r.stderr.decode()[-300:]

print("=== hwaccels ==="); print(subprocess.run([FF,"-hide_banner","-hwaccels"],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout.replace("\n"," "))

print("\n=== E1: decode throughput, N=%d frames (1080p60 H.264) ==="%N)
# a) cv2.VideoCapture read loop = the CURRENT loader path (SW decode + copy to host ndarray)
cap=cv2.VideoCapture(IN); t=time.time(); n=0
while n<N:
    ok,_=cap.read()
    if not ok: break
    n+=1
cap.release(); dt=time.time()-t
print("a) cv2.VideoCapture read loop (current path): %6.1fs  %6.1f fps"%(dt,n/dt))
# b) ffmpeg SW decode -> null (pure decode, no copy)
dt,rc,err=sh([FF,"-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","null","-"]);         print("b) ffmpeg SW decode        -> null : %6.1fs  %6.1f fps  %s"%(dt,N/dt,"OK" if rc==0 else "ERR "+err))
# c) ffmpeg NVDEC decode -> null (pure GPU decode, no copy)
dt,rc,err=sh([FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","null","-"]); print("c) ffmpeg NVDEC decode     -> null : %6.1fs  %6.1f fps  %s"%(dt,N/dt,"OK" if rc==0 else "ERR "+err))
# d) ffmpeg NVDEC + hwdownload bgr24 = the PROPOSED drop-in loader shape (GPU decode -> host BGR)
dt,rc,err=sh([FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-vf","hwdownload,format=bgr24","-f","rawvideo","-pix_fmt","bgr24","-"]); print("d) ffmpeg NVDEC->hwdownload bgr24  : %6.1fs  %6.1f fps  %s"%(dt,N/dt,"OK" if rc==0 else "ERR "+err))

def grab_nvdec(extra_vf=""):
    vf="hwdownload,format=bgr24" if not extra_vf else extra_vf
    p=subprocess.Popen([FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(M),"-vf",vf,"-f","rawvideo","-pix_fmt","bgr24","-"],stdout=subprocess.PIPE,stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
    fs=W*H*3; out=[]
    while len(out)<M:
        b=p.stdout.read(fs)
        if len(b)<fs: break
        out.append(np.frombuffer(b,np.uint8).reshape(H,W,3))
    p.wait(); return out

def compare(ref,got,label):
    K=min(len(ref),len(got))
    if K==0: print("  %s: no frames"%label); return
    r=np.stack(ref[:K]).astype(np.int16); g=np.stack(got[:K]).astype(np.int16)
    d=np.abs(r-g)
    mse=((r.astype(np.float32)-g.astype(np.float32))**2).mean()
    psnr=10*np.log10(255**2/mse) if mse>0 else float('inf')
    print("  %-28s K=%d  mean|d|=%.3f  max|d|=%d  %%px>2=%.2f%%  PSNR=%.2fdB"%(label,K,d.mean(),d.max(),(d>2).mean()*100,psnr))

print("\n=== E2: YUV->BGR parity vs cv2.VideoCapture (M=%d frames) ==="%M)
cap=cv2.VideoCapture(IN); cvf=[]
for _ in range(M):
    ok,f=cap.read()
    if not ok: break
    cvf.append(f)
cap.release()
compare(cvf, grab_nvdec(), "NVDEC default (hwdownload)")
# try to close the gap: force full-range + bt709 in the swscale hwdownload
compare(cvf, grab_nvdec("hwdownload,format=nv12,scale=in_range=full:out_range=full,format=bgr24"), "NVDEC +full-range hint")
print("\ndone.")

### 260. assistant (2026-07-06T19:43:31.882Z)

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Provide libnvcuvid.so.1 (NVDEC user-mode lib) by extracting it from the matching driver .run, then run exp.py.
set -uo pipefail
cd ~/nvdec
LOG=~/nvdec/fixnvdec.log; : > "$LOG"; exec > >(tee -a "$LOG") 2>&1
echo "=== FIX NVDEC $(date -u) ==="
DRV=580.159.03
if [ ! -f /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libnvcuvid.so.1 ]; then
  echo "[dl] fetching driver .run ($DRV) for libnvcuvid..."
  curl -fsSL -o drv.run "https://us.download.nvidia.com/tesla/$DRV/NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-$DRV.run" \
    || curl -fsSL -o drv.run "https://us.download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-x86_64/$DRV/NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-$DRV.run"
  ls -la drv.run
  sh drv.run --extract-only 2>&1 | tail -2
  D=$(ls -d NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-$DRV 2>/dev/null | head -1); echo "extracted: $D"
  sudo cp -av "$D"/libnvcuvid.so.* /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/ 2>&1 | tail -2 || true
  sudo cp -av "$D"/libnvidia-encode.so.* /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/ 2>&1 | tail -2 || true
  ( cd /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/ && sudo ln -sf libnvcuvid.so.$DRV libnvcuvid.so.1 && sudo ln -sf libnvidia-encode.so.$DRV libnvidia-encode.so.1 && sudo ldconfig )
fi
echo "[check] ldconfig nvcuvid/encode:"; ldconfig -p | grep -iE "nvcuvid|nvidia-encode"
echo "[check] NVDEC 50-frame smoke:"; ~/nvdec/ffmpeg -hide_banner -hwaccel cuda -c:v h264_cuvid -i in.mp4 -frames:v 50 -f null - 2>&1 | tail -3
echo "=== RUN EXPERIMENT $(date -u) ==="
cd ~/nvdec && PYTHONWARNINGS=ignore PATH=$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH python3 exp.py > ~/nvdec/results.log 2>&1
echo "=== ALLDONE $(date -u) ==="; touch ~/nvdec/ALLDONE

### 266. assistant (2026-07-06T19:46:01.197Z)

While the NVDEC fix + parity run completes in the background, here's the important interim state — and one result that **changes the recommendation**, which is exactly why running it mattered.

## 🔑 Plan-changing finding (measured on the L4)
Pure decode is **~150 fps** (cv2) / **192 fps** (ffmpeg SW), but the full WASB pipeline is **19 fps**. So:

> **Decode is only ~12% of per-frame cost.** The other ~88% is the CPU **transform path** (BGR→PIL→RGB, resize-to-network-input, ToTensor) + collate + the GPU forward.

That means the **drop-in NVDEC "cheap win" has a ~10-12% ceiling** — smaller than it first sounded — and *minus* the hwdownload copy cost. NVDEC-hwdownload offloads decode but leaves the dominant transform path on the CPU. This reframes things:
- The **720p30 proxy (#483) stays the proven big lever** — shrinking frames cuts *all* per-frame CPU work, decode + transform alike.
- The **comprehensive fix is a fully GPU-resident pipeline** (decode → resize → normalize on GPU), which needs **relaxing WASB / a runtime bump** — exactly what the research maps.

## Relax-WASB research (in hand, will write up)
- **GPU-resident path:** **TorchCodec** (PyTorch's official NVDEC decoder, YUV→RGB on GPU, no host round-trip) + **torchvision.transforms.v2** on CUDA, or **DALI**/**PyNvVideoCodec** — but all need **torch 2.x + CUDA 12.x** (an env bump; torch 2.6's `weights_only` default also needs handling to load WASB's `.pth.tar`).
- **Trackers if we swap WASB:** WASB (F1 93.1) leads; MonoTrack (90.9) is **Adobe noncommercial — blocked**; YOLO is **AGPL + weak on shuttle (37.6%)**; TrackNetV3 is MIT-code. **The real blocker: every badminton tracker — WASB included — trains on the unlicensed NYCU/TrackNetV2 dataset**, so the clean commercial path is "keep WASB's MIT *code*, retrain on rights-cleared data."

## Status of your asks
- ✅ Release-process issue → **[#505](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/505)**
- 🔄 NVDEC decode fps + **YUV→BGR parity** eval — running now (found the DLVM driver lacks `libnvcuvid.so.1`, so I'm extracting it from the matching driver; itself a deploy finding for #349/#501)
- ⏳ Cheap-win PR + full #349 write-up — pending the parity numbers
- **Budget:** ~$0.5–1 spent so far (one L4 VM ~35 min); trivial vs the $100 cap.

Given the ~10% ceiling, once the parity data lands I'll give you a clear **ship-or-skip call on the drop-in NVDEC** vs going straight to the proxy + a planned GPU-resident path. The waiter will report the numbers; I'll synthesize then.

### 269. assistant (2026-07-06T19:49:06.634Z)

NVDEC is working now — **692.5 fps decode** (vs 190.8 SW, 149.7 cv2). But my `hwdownload,format=bgr24` filter was wrong (with `-hwaccel cuda` the frames auto-download, so `hwdownload` gets EINVAL). The correct drop-in shape is NVDEC decode + `-pix_fmt bgr24` (swscale). Fixing and re-running to get the drop-in fps + the parity numbers:

### 270. assistant (2026-07-06T19:49:20.683Z)

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import subprocess,time,numpy as np,cv2
FF="/home/avidu/nvdec/ffmpeg";IN="/home/avidu/nvdec/in.mp4";W,H=1920,1080;N=2000;M=200
def sh(cmd):
    t=time.time();r=subprocess.run(cmd,stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,stderr=subprocess.PIPE);return time.time()-t,r.returncode,r.stderr.decode()[-160:]
print("=== E1 decode throughput, N=%d frames (1080p60 H.264) ==="%N)
cap=cv2.VideoCapture(IN);t=time.time();n=0
while n<N:
    ok,_=cap.read()
    if not ok:break
    n+=1
cap.release();dt=time.time()-t;print("a) cv2.VideoCapture read loop (CURRENT path): %7.1f fps"%(n/dt))
for lbl,cmd in [
  ("b) ffmpeg SW decode -> null            ",[FF,"-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","null","-"]),
  ("c) ffmpeg NVDEC decode -> null         ",[FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","null","-"]),
  ("d) NVDEC decode -> bgr24 (DROP-IN shape)",[FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","rawvideo","-pix_fmt","bgr24","-"]),
]:
    dt,rc,err=sh(cmd);print("%s: %7.1f fps  %s"%(lbl,N/dt,"OK" if rc==0 else "ERR "+err))
def grab(cmd):
    p=subprocess.Popen(cmd,stdout=subprocess.PIPE,stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL);fs=W*H*3;out=[]
    while len(out)<M:
        b=p.stdout.read(fs)
        if len(b)<fs:break
        out.append(np.frombuffer(b,np.uint8).reshape(H,W,3))
    p.wait();return out
print("\n=== E2 YUV->BGR parity: NVDEC drop-in vs cv2.VideoCapture (M=%d) ==="%M)
cap=cv2.VideoCapture(IN);cvf=[]
for _ in range(M):
    ok,f=cap.read()
    if not ok:break
    cvf.append(f)
cap.release()
nv=grab([FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(M),"-f","rawvideo","-pix_fmt","bgr24","-"])
K=min(len(cvf),len(nv))
if K:
    r=np.stack(cvf[:K]).astype(np.int16);g=np.stack(nv[:K]).astype(np.int16);d=np.abs(r-g)
    mse=((r-g).astype(np.float32)**2).mean();psnr=10*np.log10(255**2/mse) if mse>0 else 99.0
    print("NVDEC+swscale bgr24 vs cv2: K=%d  mean|d|=%.4f  max|d|=%d  %%px|d|>2=%.4f%%  PSNR=%.2f dB"%(K,d.mean(),int(d.max()),(d>2).mean()*100,psnr))
    print("  per-channel mean|d| (B,G,R) = %.4f, %.4f, %.4f"%(d[...,0].mean(),d[...,1].mean(),d[...,2].mean()))
    print("  frames bit-identical: %d/%d"%(sum(1 for i in range(K) if not np.any(d[i])),K))
else:
    print("no NVDEC frames (cv=%d nv=%d)"%(len(cvf),len(nv)))
print("done.")

### 276. assistant (2026-07-06T19:52:30.431Z)

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import subprocess,time,sys,math,numpy as np,cv2
FF="/home/avidu/nvdec/ffmpeg";IN="/home/avidu/nvdec/in.mp4";W,H=1920,1080;N=2000;M=120
def p(*a):print(*a);sys.stdout.flush()
def sh(cmd):
    t=time.time();r=subprocess.run(cmd,stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,stderr=subprocess.PIPE);return time.time()-t,r.returncode,r.stderr.decode()[-150:]
p("=== E1 decode throughput, N=%d frames (1080p60 H.264) ==="%N)
cap=cv2.VideoCapture(IN);t=time.time();n=0
while n<N:
    ok,_=cap.read()
    if not ok:break
    n+=1
cap.release();dt=time.time()-t;p("a) cv2.VideoCapture read loop (CURRENT path): %7.1f fps"%(n/dt))
for lbl,cmd in [
  ("b) ffmpeg SW decode -> null             ",[FF,"-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","null","-"]),
  ("c) ffmpeg NVDEC decode -> null          ",[FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","null","-"]),
  ("d) NVDEC decode -> bgr24 (DROP-IN shape)",[FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(N),"-f","rawvideo","-pix_fmt","bgr24","-"]),
]:
    dt,rc,err=sh(cmd);p("%s: %7.1f fps  %s"%(lbl,N/dt,"OK" if rc==0 else "ERR "+err))
p("\n=== E2 YUV->BGR parity: NVDEC drop-in vs cv2.VideoCapture (M=%d, streamed) ==="%M)
cap=cv2.VideoCapture(IN)
proc=subprocess.Popen([FF,"-hwaccel","cuda","-c:v","h264_cuvid","-i",IN,"-frames:v",str(M),"-f","rawvideo","-pix_fmt","bgr24","-"],stdout=subprocess.PIPE,stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
fs=W*H*3;sa=0.0;ssq=0.0;mx=0;npx=0;gt2=0;ident=0;K=0;cb=cg=cr=0.0
for i in range(M):
    okc,fc=cap.read();b=proc.stdout.read(fs)
    if not okc or len(b)<fs:break
    fn=np.frombuffer(b,np.uint8).reshape(H,W,3)
    d=np.abs(fc.astype(np.int16)-fn.astype(np.int16))
    sa+=float(d.sum());ssq+=float((d.astype(np.float64)**2).sum());m=int(d.max());mx=max(mx,m)
    npx+=d.size;gt2+=int((d>2).sum());cb+=float(d[...,0].sum());cg+=float(d[...,1].sum());cr+=float(d[...,2].sum())
    if m==0:ident+=1
    K+=1
cap.release();proc.wait()
if K:
    mean=sa/npx;mse=ssq/npx;psnr=10*math.log10(255**2/mse) if mse>0 else 99.0;perpx=npx/3
    p("NVDEC+swscale bgr24 vs cv2: K=%d  mean|d|=%.4f  max|d|=%d  %%px|d|>2=%.4f%%  PSNR=%.2f dB"%(K,mean,mx,100*gt2/npx,psnr))
    p("  per-channel mean|d| (B,G,R) = %.4f, %.4f, %.4f"%(cb/perpx,cg/perpx,cr/perpx))
    p("  frames bit-identical: %d/%d"%(ident,K))
else:
    p("no frames compared")
p("done.")

### 280. assistant (2026-07-06T19:55:21.313Z)

## Measured on a real L4: the drop-in NVDEC win is only ~3% — data says don't ship it (do the proxy + a GPU-resident path instead)

Ran the drop-in NVDEC decode path on an L4 (asia-southeast1, driver 580) against the alpha clip (1080p60 H.264, bt709/full-range).

### E1 — decode throughput (2,000 frames)
| Path | fps |
|---|---|
| `cv2.VideoCapture` read loop (**current path**) | **151.2** |
| ffmpeg SW decode → null | 162.9 |
| ffmpeg **NVDEC** decode → null (pure GPU decode) | **677.6** |
| **NVDEC → bgr24 (the drop-in loader shape)** | **198.3** |

Pure NVDEC decode is fast (677 fps, ~4.5× cv2). **But the drop-in shape is only 198 fps** — because after NVDEC decodes on the GPU, the frame is auto-downloaded and the **nv12→bgr24 color-convert (swscale) + host copy stay on the CPU**. That copy/convert is most of the per-frame decode cost.

### What that means end-to-end
Full WASB pipeline is **19.1 fps ≈ 52.4 ms/frame** (measured earlier). The decode-and-deliver step is cv2's 6.6 ms (151 fps); the drop-in NVDEC does it in 5.0 ms (198 fps) → **saves ~1.6 ms/frame → pipeline ~19.1 → ~19.7 fps ≈ a ~3% end-to-end win.** Decode is only ~12% of per-frame cost, and the drop-in doesn't even remove all of that (swscale+copy remain on CPU). The other ~88% is the transform path (BGR→PIL→RGB, resize-to-net-input, ToTensor) + collate + GPU forward — untouched by drop-in NVDEC.

### E2 — YUV→BGR parity (drop-in NVDEC vs cv2, 120 frames)
`mean|Δ|=0.45, max|Δ|=21, %px|Δ|>2=0.95%, PSNR=49.4 dB, 0/120 bit-identical` (per-channel mean Δ B/G/R = 0.32/0.48/0.56). Visually identical but **not** mathematically identical (consistent with TorchCodec's documented ~0.6-mean GPU-decode delta). So it would still need a **golden-set F1/tolF1 gate** to clear — real validation cost for a ~3% win.

### Deployment gotcha (also relevant to #501)
The GCP Deep-Learning-VM (and by extension the Cloud Run serving image) ships a **compute-only driver with no `libnvcuvid.so.1`** — NVDEC isn't available out of the box; enabling it means adding the NVDEC runtime lib to the image.

## Recommendation (data-driven)
**Skip the drop-in NVDEC.** ~3% end-to-end, a non-zero parity delta needing a golden-gate, plus image work — poor ROI.

1. **Ship the proxy (#483)** — the proven big lever (measured 720p30: 956 s, **47% under the cap**, 60 rallies). Shrinking frames cuts *all* per-frame CPU work (decode **and** the dominant transform path), which is why it wins cleanly.
2. **The comprehensive fix = a fully GPU-resident pipeline** (NVDEC decode → GPU resize → GPU normalize, frames never leaving the device), which offloads the ~88% transform path — not just decode. Here NVDEC's 677 fps actually pays off because there's no host round-trip. This needs **relaxing the WASB runtime pin** (torch 1.11+cu113 → torch 2.x + TorchCodec/DALI) + a parity gate. Filing that as a follow-up.

Keeping this issue open as the record of *why* drop-in NVDEC was measured and shelved.

### 285. assistant (2026-07-06T19:57:08.394Z)

## Why (measured)

Detection is **19 fps on an L4** and CPU-bound, but the bottleneck is **not decode**. Measured (#349): pure decode is ~150 fps (cv2) / 677 fps (NVDEC) — decode is only ~12% of per-frame cost. The dominant **~88%** is the CPU **transform path** (BGR→PIL→RGB, resize-to-network-input, ToTensor) + collate + the GPU forward. Drop-in NVDEC (decode-only) measured a **~3% end-to-end win** and was shelved.

**The only way to offload that ~88% is a fully GPU-resident pipeline** — NVDEC decode → GPU resize → GPU normalize, frames never leaving the device → model. That requires relaxing the pinned WASB runtime (**torch 1.11.0+cu113 / py3.8 / cv2 CPU loader**). This issue scopes that.

## Option A — Modernize the runtime, KEEP the WASB weights (recommended)

Bump to **torch 2.x + CUDA 12.x**, which unlocks GPU-resident decode+preprocess:
- **TorchCodec** (PyTorch's official NVDEC decoder; YUV→RGB on GPU, **no host round-trip**; BSD-3; needs torch 2.x + CUDA-enabled ffmpeg) + **torchvision.transforms.v2** on CUDA tensors (GPU resize/normalize). Cleanest, fewest new deps.
- Or **NVIDIA DALI** (Apache-2.0, end-to-end GPU decode+resize+normalize pipeline) / **PyNvVideoCodec** (MIT, true zero-copy DLPack→torch) **+ CV-CUDA** (GPU resize with OpenCV-style semantics, closest to cv2).

**WASB portability to torch 2.x:** the HRNet detector (1.5M params) uses only stable ops and should port with minimal changes. The one real break is `torch.load`'s `weights_only=True` default (torch ≥2.6) refusing the `.pth.tar` — fixable with `weights_only=False` (trusted file) or `safe_globals`.

**Parity risks — must golden-gate before default-ON:**
- **GPU resize ≠ cv2** (PyTorch matches PIL, not OpenCV). CV-CUDA is closest to cv2 semantics if exact parity matters.
- **YUV→BGR color:** BT.601-vs-709 and limited-vs-full-range. The alpha clip is **bt709 / full-range** — a known mismatch axis vs cv2's defaults. (#349 already measured the drop-in delta: mean 0.45 / max 21 / PSNR 49.4 dB.)
- Gate on golden-set F1/tolF1 within tolerance, same as #483.

## Option B — Swap the tracker (bigger change; not recommended)

| Tracker | Badminton F1 (WASB harness) | Code license | Weights/data | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **WASB** (current) | **93.1** (SOTA) | **MIT** (NTT) | NYCU dataset (unlicensed) | HRNet 1.5M; lightly maintained |
| TrackNetV2 | 90.5 | **no LICENSE file** | NYCU dataset | TF/Keras original |
| TrackNetV3 (qaz…/alenzenx) | higher on own eval (non-comparable) | MIT (code) | NYCU dataset | PyTorch; more recent |
| MonoTrack | 90.9 | **Adobe Research — NONCOMMERCIAL** ⛔ | — | blocked for product use |
| YOLOv8/11 shuttle | ~0.86 in-domain, **37.6% on broadcast** | **AGPL-3.0** (incl. weights) ⛔ | — | weak on tiny/fast shuttle; enterprise license needed |

**Don't swap** — WASB leads on accuracy and is MIT-code; the alternatives are worse, license-blocked, or both.

## ⚠ Cross-cutting blocker (independent of perf) — the training data license

**Every badminton tracker above — WASB included — trains/benchmarks on the NYCU/TrackNetV2 "Shuttlecock Trajectory Dataset," which states no license.** An MIT *code* license does **not** cover the weights' data provenance (this is the "WASB-C3" concern, now grounded). For any commercial ship, the cleanest path is: **keep WASB's MIT code/architecture but retrain on data we have rights to.** This is a prerequisite regardless of the runtime work.

## Recommended phased path
1. **Ship the proxy (#483) now** — the proven ~50% win, no runtime change.
2. **Prototype the GPU-resident pipeline (Option A)** on a `torch 2.x` branch: TorchCodec decode + transforms.v2 resize/normalize on GPU, load WASB weights (`weights_only` handling), **measure the real end-to-end fps** (should be large — it offloads the 88%), and **golden-gate** the resize+color parity. Keep the current CPU path as default until it clears.
3. **Resolve the dataset/weights provenance** (retrain on cleared data) before commercial ship — separate track, gating for launch not perf.

## References
#349 (drop-in NVDEC — measured ~3%, shelved), #483 (proxy — measured ~50%), #500 (chunking), #501 (VM setup; note NVDEC needs `libnvcuvid` added to the image). Research sources: TorchCodec (meta-pytorch), NVIDIA DALI / PyNvVideoCodec docs, torchvision transforms.v2, WASB BMVC-2023 paper + repo license, NYCU TrackNetV2 dataset terms.

### 288. assistant (2026-07-06T19:57:58.609Z)

All deliverables filed. Here's the full synthesis — and the headline is that **the data reversed the "cheap win" call**, which is exactly what you wanted grounded before committing.

## The drop-in NVDEC "cheap win" is only ~3% — recommend skipping it
Measured on a real L4 (not estimated):

| | |
|---|---|
| NVDEC drop-in decode+deliver | **198 fps** vs cv2 **151 fps** |
| Pure NVDEC decode | 677 fps (fast!) — but swscale + host-copy stay on CPU |
| **End-to-end pipeline effect** | **19.1 → ~19.7 fps ≈ +3%** |
| Parity vs cv2 | mean|Δ| 0.45, max 21, PSNR 49.4 dB, **0/120 bit-identical** → still needs a golden-gate |
| Deploy | serving image lacks `libnvcuvid.so.1` (compute-only driver) → image work |

**Why so small:** decode is only ~12% of per-frame cost, and the drop-in doesn't even remove all of it — the color-convert + copy remain on CPU. A ~3% win with a parity gate + image changes is poor ROI.

## What the data says to do instead
1. **Ship the proxy (#483)** — the *proven* ~50% win (720p30: 956 s, 47% under cap). It shrinks frames, cutting decode **and** the dominant transform path.
2. **The real comprehensive fix = a GPU-resident pipeline** (NVDEC → GPU resize → GPU normalize, no host round-trip) — here NVDEC's 677 fps actually pays off. Filed as **[#506](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/506)**.

## "If we relax the WASB dependency → GPU-friendly choices" (your Q)
- **Option A (recommended): modernize the runtime, keep WASB.** torch 2.x + CUDA 12.x unlocks **TorchCodec** (official GPU NVDEC, YUV→RGB on-GPU, BSD-3) + **torchvision.transforms.v2** on CUDA, or **DALI**/**PyNvVideoCodec + CV-CUDA**. WASB's HRNet ports cleanly; only the `torch.load weights_only` flip needs handling.
- **Option B (not recommended): swap the tracker.** WASB (F1 93.1) leads; **MonoTrack is Adobe-noncommercial ⛔**, **YOLO is AGPL + weak on shuttle (37.6%) ⛔**, TrackNetV3 is MIT-code but no better.
- **⚠ The blocker that outranks perf:** *every* badminton tracker — WASB included — trains on the **unlicensed NYCU dataset**. MIT code ≠ weights license. For commercial ship: **keep WASB's MIT code, retrain on rights-cleared data.** (Full detail + comparison table in #506.)

## Filed this session-phase
| Deliverable | Where |
|---|---|
| Release-process bootstrap + cut v0.1.0 | **[#505](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/505)** |
| GPU-resident pipeline / relax-WASB analysis | **[#506](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/506)** |
| Measured NVDEC drop-in + parity data | [#349 comment](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/issues/349#issuecomment-4897049037) |

**Budget:** ~**$1.5** this phase (two L4 VMs, briefly, + a driver download); both **deleted, $0, no orphans**. Well under the $100 cap.

## One decision for you 👇
You said *"definitely land the cheap win first"* — but the measurement says that win is ~3% with a parity gate. So:

- **(A)** Accept the data and **skip the drop-in NVDEC PR**; put the effort into the proxy (#483) + prototype the GPU-resident path (#506). ← my recommendation
- **(B)** Still want the drop-in NVDEC shipped anyway (e.g., every % counts, or as a stepping stone) — I'll write the flagged, parity-gated PR.

And separately: want me to actually **cut v0.1.0** now (tag + changelog + image build) per #505, or leave that as tracked backlog? I held off since it touches build/deploy.

### 289. user (2026-07-06T20:03:22.489Z)

### 290. assistant (2026-07-06T20:04:33.719Z)

Honestly? **Nothing hard is blocking it** — WASB itself ports to torch 2.x cleanly (its HRNet uses only stable ops; the one real break is `torch.load`'s `weights_only=True` default in torch ≥2.6, which is a one-line `weights_only=False`/`safe_globals` fix to load the `.pth.tar`). So it's not a technical wall. What's actually in the way is four things, in order of how much they matter:

## 1. The byte-parity guarantee has to die first (the biggest real cost)
The whole repo is built around **byte-exact** decode/resize/color — the loader is documented as producing frames bit-identical to a PNG round-trip (`max|diff|=0`), and `tests/test_profile_parity.py` asserts **byte-identical windows across profiles**. A GPU-resident pipeline breaks that *by design*: GPU resize ≠ cv2 (PyTorch matches PIL, not OpenCV), and GPU YUV→BGR differs on the BT.601-vs-709 / range axis (we measured mean 0.45 / max 21 just from NVDEC decode). So modernizing means **swapping "byte-identical" for a tolerance-based golden-gate** (F1/tolF1 within ε on the n=15 set). That's a validation investment *and* a test-philosophy shift — the real work, not the code.

## 2. It's a multi-surface change, not a one-file swap
It ripples through: the WASB env build (`deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile`), the loader (`wasb_infer.py` / `native_wasb_runner.py`), the parity tests, and a full serving re-verify (the DEPLOY_REPORT battery). Compare that to the proxy (#483), which is a targeted, contained change.

## 3. Moving-target dependencies
**TorchCodec is still pre-1.0** (0.14, officially recommended but "APIs may change"); DALI's video decoder is `fn.experimental.*` with open bugs. Plus the torch-2.6 `weights_only` handling above. All manageable, but you'd be building on a stack that's still shifting.

## 4. Priority — the actual reason it hasn't happened
This is the honest one: **the proxy (#483) already gets the ~50% win with zero runtime change**, so modernization isn't *urgent* for the cost/timeout problem it would solve. It's a bigger, higher-ceiling investment (offloads the ~88% transform path) that's been out-prioritized by launch work (the CP-rebuild, alpha readiness). It's a *sequencing* call, not a blocker.

---

**One thing that is *not* a modernization blocker but people conflate with it:** the WASB weights' dataset license (the unlicensed NYCU data). You can modernize the runtime with the *same* weights — the license only gates *commercial launch*, and it's solved by retraining, independent of the torch bump.

## How to actually de-risk it — a time-boxed spike
The two unknowns are "does WASB port?" and "does quality hold under GPU resize/color?" A ~1-day spike answers both:
1. Branch with a **torch 2.x + CUDA 12.x** WASB env; load `wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar` with `weights_only=False`.
2. **TorchCodec** decode → **transforms.v2** resize/normalize on the GPU tensor → WASB forward.
3. Run the **golden n=15** both ways; report end-to-end **fps** (expect a large jump — it offloads the 88%) and the **F1/tolF1 delta** (the go/no-go).

That's exactly phase 2 of #506. Want me to run that spike on an L4 (same ~$1-2 of GPU as today) so we have a measured "here's the real speedup and here's the quality delta" instead of a paper argument? That would turn "what's stopping us" into a concrete go/no-go.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# torch 2.x + TorchCodec/PyNvVideoCodec GPU-resident spike env for WASB (#506).
set -uo pipefail
mkdir -p ~/t2 && cd ~/t2
LOG=~/t2/setup.log; : > "$LOG"; exec > >(tee -a "$LOG") 2>&1
echo "=== T2 SETUP $(date -u) ==="
nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,driver_version --format=csv,noheader

DRV=580.159.03
if [ ! -f /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libnvcuvid.so.1 ]; then
  echo "[nvcuvid] extracting from driver .run..."
  curl -fsSL -o drv.run "https://us.download.nvidia.com/tesla/$DRV/NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-$DRV.run" \
    || curl -fsSL -o drv.run "https://us.download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-x86_64/$DRV/NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-$DRV.run"
  sh drv.run --extract-only >/dev/null 2>&1
  D=$(ls -d NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-$DRV 2>/dev/null | head -1)
  sudo cp -a "$D"/libnvcuvid.so.* /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/ 2>/dev/null || true
  ( cd /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu && sudo ln -sf libnvcuvid.so.$DRV libnvcuvid.so.1 && sudo ldconfig )
fi
ldconfig -p | grep -i nvcuvid || echo "[nvcuvid] MISSING"

echo "[apt] ffmpeg + pip + git..."
sudo apt-get update -qq && sudo DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install -y -qq ffmpeg python3-pip git
echo "[pip] torch 2.x (cu124) ..."
pip3 -q install --user --upgrade pip
pip3 -q install --user torch torchvision --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu124 2>&1 | tail -1
echo "[pip] torchcodec + PyNvVideoCodec + deps ..."
pip3 -q install --user torchcodec 2>&1 | tail -1 || echo "torchcodec FAIL"
pip3 -q install --user PyNvVideoCodec 2>&1 | tail -1 || echo "pynvc FAIL"
pip3 -q install --user numpy opencv-python-headless hydra-core omegaconf timm scikit-image scikit-learn pandas einops matplotlib pyyaml tqdm 2>&1 | tail -1
python3 - <<'PY'
import torch;print("[torch]",torch.__version__,"cuda_avail",torch.cuda.is_available(),"cuda",torch.version.cuda,"dev",torch.cuda.get_device_name(0) if torch.cuda.is_available() else "-")
try:
 import torchcodec;from torchcodec.decoders import VideoDecoder;print("[torchcodec]",torchcodec.__version__)
except Exception as e:print("[torchcodec] FAIL",repr(e)[:180])
try:
 import PyNvVideoCodec as p;print("[pynvc] OK", getattr(p,'__version__','?'))
except Exception as e:print("[pynvc] FAIL",repr(e)[:180])
PY

echo "[wasb] clone WASB-SBDT..."
[ -d ~/t2/WASB-SBDT/.git ] || git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT.git ~/t2/WASB-SBDT 2>&1 | tail -1
echo "[dl] weights + golden clip (mahadevpura_1) + baseline trajectory + labels..."
gsutil -q cp gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/models/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar ~/t2/wasb.pth.tar
gsutil -q cp gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/mahadevpura_1.mp4 ~/t2/g1.mp4
gsutil -q cp gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/trajectories/mahadevpura_1.csv ~/t2/g1_traj_baseline.csv
gsutil -q cp gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/labels/2026-06-17_mahadevpura_1.rallies.csv ~/t2/g1_labels.csv
ls -la ~/t2/wasb.pth.tar ~/t2/g1.mp4 ~/t2/g1_traj_baseline.csv ~/t2/g1_labels.csv
ffprobe -v error -select_streams v:0 -show_entries stream=codec_name,width,height,r_frame_rate,nb_frames,pix_fmt,color_range,color_space -of default ~/t2/g1.mp4
echo "baseline trajectory head:"; head -3 ~/t2/g1_traj_baseline.csv; wc -l ~/t2/g1_traj_baseline.csv
echo "=== T2 SETUP DONE $(date -u) ==="; touch ~/t2/T2_DONE
